## Government and public services

### Civic administration

Main article: [Civic administration of Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civic_administration_of_Kolkata)



[Calcutta High Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calcutta_High_Court)



[General Post Office, Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Post_Office,_Kolkata)

Kolkata is administered by several government agencies. The [Kolkata Municipal Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_Municipal_Corporation), or KMC, oversees and manages the civic infrastructure of the city's 16 boroughs, which together encompass 144 wards.[^cite_note-kmcbasicstat-140] Each ward elects a councillor to the KMC. Each borough has a committee of councillors, each of whom is elected to represent a ward. By means of the borough committees, the corporation undertakes urban planning and maintains roads, government-aided schools, hospitals, and municipal markets.[^cite_note-kmcfunction-164] As Kolkata's apex body, the corporation discharges its functions through the mayor-in-council, which comprises a [mayor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_Kolkata), a deputy mayor, and ten other elected members of the KMC.[^cite_note-kmcstructure-165] The functions of the KMC include water supply, drainage and sewerage, sanitation, solid waste management, street lighting, and building regulation.[^cite_note-kmcfunction-164]

Kolkata's administrative agencies have areas of jurisdiction that do not coincide. Listed in ascending order by area, they are: [Kolkata district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_district); the [Kolkata Police area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divisions_of_Kolkata_Police) and the Kolkata Municipal Corporation area, or "Kolkata city";[^cite_note-166] and the [Kolkata metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_metropolitan_area), which is the city's urban agglomeration. The agency overseeing the latter, the [Kolkata Metropolitan Development Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_Metropolitan_Development_Authority), is responsible for the [statutory planning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statutory_planning) and development of greater Kolkata.[^cite_note-167] The Kolkata Municipal Corporation was ranked first out of 21 cities for best governance and administrative practices in India in 2014. It scored 4.0 on 10 compared to the national average of 3.3.[^cite_note-168]

The Kolkata Port Trust, an agency of the central government, manages the city's river port. The city has an apolitical [titular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titular_ruler) post, that of the [Sheriff of Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheriff_of_Kolkata), which presides over various city-related functions and conferences.[^cite_note-169]

As the seat of the [Government of West Bengal](6629274.md), Kolkata is home to not only the offices of the local governing agencies, but also the [West Bengal Legislative Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Bengal_Legislative_Assembly); the state secretariat, which is housed in the Writers' Building; and the [Calcutta High Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calcutta_High_Court). Most government establishments and institutions are housed in the centre of the city in B. B. D. Bagh (formerly known as Dalhousie Square). The Calcutta High Court is the oldest [High Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Courts_of_India) in India. It was preceded by the [Supreme Court of Judicature at Fort William](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Judicature_at_Fort_William) which was established in 1774. The Calcutta High Court has jurisdiction over the state of [West Bengal](34040.md) and the [Union Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Territory) of the [Andaman and Nicobar Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andaman_and_Nicobar_Islands). Kolkata has lower courts: the Court of Small Causes and the City Civil Court decide civil matters; the [Sessions Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sessions_Court) rules in criminal cases.[^cite_note-170][^cite_note-171][^cite_note-172] The [Kolkata Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_Police), headed by a police commissioner, is overseen by the [West Bengal Ministry of Home Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Home_Affairs_\(West_Bengal\)).[^cite_note-173][^cite_note-174] The Kolkata district elects two representatives to India's lower house, the [Lok Sabha](275456.md), and 11 representatives to the state legislative assembly.[^cite_note-delimitgazzette-175] The Kolkata police district registered 15,510 [Indian Penal Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Penal_Code) cases in 2010, the 8th-highest total in the country.[^cite_note-ncrb2-176] In 2010, the crime rate was 117.3 per 100,000, below the national rate of 187.6; it was the lowest rate among India's largest cities.[^cite_note-ncrb10megacities-177]

### Utility services

The Kolkata Municipal Corporation supplies the city with potable water that is sourced from the Hooghly River;[^cite_note-178] most of it is treated and purified at the Palta pumping station located in North 24 Parganas district.[^cite_note-179] Roughly 95% of the 4,000 tonnes of refuse produced daily by the city is transported to the dumping grounds in [Dhapa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhapa,_India), which is east of the town.[^cite_note-180][^cite_note-181] To promote the recycling of garbage and sewer water, agriculture is encouraged on the dumping grounds.[^cite_note-UN-182] Parts of the city lack proper sewerage, leading to unsanitary methods of waste disposal.[^cite_note-ESS-95]

In 1856, the Bengal Government appointed [George Turnbull](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Turnbull_\(engineer\)) to be the Commissioner of Drainage and Sewerage to improve the city's sewerage. Turnbull's main job was to be the Chief Engineer of the [East Indian Railway Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Indian_Railway_Company) responsible for building the first railway 541 miles (871 km) from [Howrah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Howrah) to [Varanasi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varanasi) (then [Benares](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benares)).

Electricity is supplied by the privately operated [Calcutta Electric Supply Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CESC_Limited), or CESC, to the city proper; the [West Bengal State Electricity Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Bengal_State_Electricity_Board) supplies it in the suburbs.[^cite_note-183][^cite_note-184] Fire services are handled by the [West Bengal Fire Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Bengal_Fire_Service), a state agency.[^cite_note-fireservice-185] As of 2012, the city had 16 fire stations.[^cite_note-186]

State-owned [Bharat Sanchar Nigam Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharat_Sanchar_Nigam_Limited), or BSNL, as well as private enterprises, among them [Vodafone Idea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vodafone_Idea), [Bharti Airtel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharti_Airtel), [Reliance Jio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jio) are the leading telephone and cell phone service providers in the city.[^cite_note-trai_ar_11_wire-187]<sup>: 25–26 </sup><sup>: 179 </sup> with Kolkata being the first city in India to have cell phone and [4G](486547.md) connectivity, the [GSM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GSM) and [CDMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Code_division_multiple_access) cellular coverage is extensive.[^cite_note-188][^cite_note-189] As of 2010, Kolkata has 7 percent of the total broadband internet consumers in India; BSNL, [VSNL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VSNL), [Tata Indicom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tata_Indicom), Sify, [Hathway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hathway), Airtel, and Jio are among the main vendors.[^cite_note-190][^cite_note-191]

### Military and diplomatic establishments



Fort William, Kolkata

The [Eastern Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Command_\(India\)) of the [Indian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Army) is based in the city. Being one of India's major city and the largest city in eastern and north-eastern India, Kolkata hosts diplomatic missions of many countries such as [Australia, Bangladesh, Bhutan, Canada, People's Republic of China, France, Germany, Italy, Japan, Myanmar, Nepal, Russia, Sri Lanka, Switzerland, Thailand, United Kingdom and United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_in_India). The [U.S Consulate in Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consulate_General_of_the_United_States,_Kolkata) is the [US Department of State](31975.md)'s second-oldest consulate and dates from 19 November 1792.[^cite_note-192] The diplomatic representation of more than 65 countries and international organizations is present in Kolkata as consulate offices, honorary consulate offices, cultural centres, deputy high commission and economic section, and trade representation offices.[^cite_note-193]

## Transport

Main article: [Transport in Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Kolkata)

Public transport is provided by the [Kolkata Suburban Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_Suburban_Railway), the [Kolkata Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_Metro), [trams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trams_in_Kolkata), [rickshaws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rickshaws), taxis and buses. The suburban rail network connects the city's distant suburbs.

### Rail

#### Rapid transit

Main article: [Kolkata Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_Metro)



[Kolkata Metro Green Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_Metro_Green_Line)

[Kolkata Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_Metro) is the rapid transit system of Kolkat. According to a 2013 survey conducted by the [International Association of Public Transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Association_of_Public_Transport), in terms of a public transport system, Kolkata ranks top among the six Indian cities surveyed.[^cite_note-194][^cite_note-195] The Kolkata Metro, in operation since 1984, is the oldest underground mass transit system in India.[^cite_note-196] The fully operational blue line spans the north–south length through the middle of the city. In 2020, part of the second line was inaugurated to cover part of [Salt Lake City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bidhannagar), Kolkata metro area. This east–west green line connects two satellite cities of Kolkata, namely Salt Lake and Howrah. Other operational lines are the Purple line and Orange line.[^cite_note-197]

#### Commuter rail

Main article: [Kolkata Suburban Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_Suburban_Railway)



[Howrah Junction railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Howrah_Junction_railway_station), the largest and busiest railway complex in India (as of 2024)

[Kolkata Suburban Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_Suburban_Railway) is the [largest and second busiest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_suburban_and_commuter_rail_systems) suburban railway network in the country by number of stations and track length, and also one of the largest in the world. Kolkata has five long-distance inter-city railway stations, located at [Howrah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Howrah_railway_station) (the largest and busiest railway complex in India, as of 2024), [Sealdah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sealdah_railway_station) (2nd busiest in India, as of 2024), [Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_railway_station), [Shalimar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shalimar_railway_station) and [Santragachi Junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santragachi_Junction_railway_station), which connect Kolkata by rail to most cities in West Bengal and to other major cities in India.[^cite_note-198] The city serves as the headquarters of three railway zones out of eighteen of the [Indian Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Railways) regional divisions namely the Kolkata Metro, [Eastern Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Railway_zone) and the [South Eastern Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Eastern_Railway_zone).[^cite_note-railzones-199] Kolkata has international rail connectivity with [Dhaka](56656.md), the capital of Bangladesh.[^cite_note-200][^cite_note-201][^cite_note-202]

### Tram

Main article: [Trams in Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trams_in_Kolkata)



[Kolkata tramway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trams_in_Kolkata) is the only operational [tramway](30733.md) in India. It was founded in 1902 as the [Calcutta Tramways Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calcutta_Tramways_Company).

Kolkata is the only Indian city with a tram network, which was operated by the [Calcutta Tramways Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calcutta_Tramways_Company). It has now amalgamated to [West Bengal Transport Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Bengal_Transport_Corporation).[^cite_note-onlytram-203] There are three operational routes: [Tollygunge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tollygunge) to [Ballygunge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballygunge), [Gariahat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gariahat) to [Esplanade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esplanade,_Kolkata), [Shyambazar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shyambazar) to [Esplanade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esplanade,_Kolkata). Trams are environment friendly but due to slow-moving and traffic congestion, tram attracts less passengers. Water-logging, caused by heavy rains during the [summer monsoon](31096097.md), sometimes interrupts transportation networks.[^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205]

### Roads and expressways

Main article: [List of roads in Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_roads_in_Kolkata)

See also: [List of expressways in West Bengal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_expressways_in_West_Bengal)



Red Road, a major six-lane road in Central Kolkata

Kolkata along its [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_Metropolitan_Area) is home to the second largest road network in India. As of 2022, total road network in the city's [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_metropolitan_area) is 4,018 km (2,497 mi),[^cite_note-206] while the city proper has road network of 1,850 km (1,150 mi).[^cite_note-auto1-207] The city has witnessed a steady increase in the number of registered vehicles: from 17 lakhs in 2019 to 21 lakhs in 2022, an 18.52 per cent jump.[^cite_note-208] With 2,448 vehicles per kilometre of road, Kolkata has the highest car density in India.[^cite_note-auto1-207] This leads major traffic congestion. The city's main bus terminals are located at [Esplanade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esplanade,_Kolkata) and [Howrah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Howrah).[^cite_note-209] The Kolkata–Delhi and Kolkata–[Chennai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai) prongs of the [Golden Quadrilateral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Quadrilateral), and [National Highway 12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highway_12_\(India\)) start from the outskirts of the city.[^cite_note-210]

As of 2024, Kolkata has one state expressway and two national [expressways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expressways_of_India), all in its metropolitan area. [Kalyani Expressway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalyani_Expressway) is only state expressway, which is partially operational and partially under construction. The national expressways are [Belghoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belghoria_Expressway) (part of [AH1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AH1) and [NH12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highway_12_\(India\))), operational and [Kona Expressway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kona_Expressway) (part of NH12), at grade road operational but elevated corridor under construction. Some national expressways are planned or in various stages of construction to connect directly with many major metropolises and cities of India, such as [Varanasi–Kolkata Expressway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varanasi%E2%80%93Kolkata_Expressway)[^cite_note-211] and Patna Kolkata Expressway.[^cite_note-212]

Kolkata has international road connectivity to [Dhaka](56656.md), [Bangladesh](3454.md) by [Jessore Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jessore_Road);[^cite_note-213] to [Bangkok](56615.md), [Thailand](30128.md) and [Myanmar](19457.md) by Kolkata-Thailand-Bangkok Trilateral Highway (an extension of [IMT Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India%E2%80%93Myanmar%E2%80%93Thailand_Trilateral_Highway))[^cite_note-214] and to [Nepal](171166.md) and [Bhutan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhutan) by NH12 and proposed [Haldia–Raxaul Expressway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haldia%E2%80%93Raxaul_Expressway).



The yellow taxi remains a favourite mode of transportation despite the foray of [rideshare companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ridesharing_company) in the transport market.

Hired public conveyances include [auto rickshaws](593765.md), which often ply specific routes, and yellow metered taxis. Almost all of Kolkata's taxis are antiquated [Hindustan Ambassadors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindustan_Ambassador) by make; newer air-conditioned [radio taxis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxicab#Dispatching) are in service as well.[^cite_note-215][^cite_note-216] In parts of the city, [cycle rickshaws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cycle_rickshaw) and [hand-pulled rickshaws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulled_rickshaw) are used by the public for short trips.[^cite_note-217]

### Air



[Netaji Subhas Chandra Bose International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netaji_Subhas_Chandra_Bose_International_Airport)

[Netaji Subhas Chandra Bose International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netaji_Subhas_Chandra_Bose_International_Airport), located in Dum Dum, about 16 km (9.9 mi) north-east of the city centre, operates domestic and international flights. In 2013, the airport was upgraded to handle increased air traffic.[^cite_note-218][^cite_note-219]

### Water

The [Port of Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Kolkata), established in 1870, is India's oldest and the only major river port.[^cite_note-ibmport-220] The Kolkata Port Trust manages docks in Kolkata and [Haldia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haldia).[^cite_note-dockport-221] The port hosts passenger services to [Port Blair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Blair), capital of the Andaman and Nicobar Islands; freighter service to ports throughout India and around the world is operated by the [Shipping Corporation of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shipping_Corporation_of_India).[^cite_note-ibmport-220][^cite_note-222] Ferry services connect Kolkata with its twin city of [Howrah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Howrah), located across the Hooghly River.[^cite_note-223][^cite_note-224]

## Healthcare

See also: [Healthcare in Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare_in_Kolkata)



[Medical College and Hospital, Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_College_and_Hospital,_Kolkata)



[IPGMER and SSKM Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPGMER_and_SSKM_Hospital)

As of 2011, the healthcare system in Kolkata consists of 48 government hospitals, mostly under the [Department of Health & Family Welfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Health_&_Family_Welfare_\(West_Bengal\)), Government of West Bengal, and 366 private medical establishments;[^cite_note-hospital2011-225] these establishments provide the city with 27,687 hospital beds.[^cite_note-hospital2011-225] For every 10,000 people in the city, there are 61.7 hospital beds,[^cite_note-hospital-226] which is higher than the national average of 9 hospital beds per 10,000.[^cite_note-227] Ten [medical and dental colleges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Kolkata#Medical_Colleges) are located in the Kolkata metropolitan area which act as [tertiary referral hospitals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertiary_referral_hospital) in the state.[^cite_note-228][^cite_note-229] The [Calcutta Medical College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calcutta_Medical_College), founded in 1835, was the first institution in Asia to teach modern medicine.[^cite_note-calmed-230] However, these facilities are inadequate to meet the healthcare needs of the city.[^cite_note-231][^cite_note-232][^cite_note-233] More than 78% in Kolkata prefer the private medical sector over the public medical sector,[^cite_note-healthsurvey-136]<sup>: 109 </sup> due to the overburdening of the public health sector, the lack of a nearby facility, and excessive waiting times at government facilities.[^cite_note-healthsurvey-136]<sup>: 61 </sup>

According to the Indian 2005 National Family Health Survey, only a small proportion of Kolkata households were covered under any health scheme or [health insurance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_insurance).[^cite_note-healthsurvey-136]<sup>: 41 </sup> The [total fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_fertility_rate) in Kolkata was 1.4, the lowest among the eight cities surveyed.[^cite_note-healthsurvey-136]<sup>: 45 </sup> In Kolkata, 77% of the married women used [contraceptives](18978770.md), which was the highest among the cities surveyed, but use of modern contraceptive methods was the lowest (46%).[^cite_note-healthsurvey-136]<sup>: 47 </sup> The [infant mortality rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant_mortality_rate) in Kolkata was 41 per 1,000 live births, and the mortality rate for children under five was 49 per 1,000 live births.[^cite_note-healthsurvey-136]<sup>: 48 </sup>

Among the surveyed cities, Kolkata stood second (5%) for children who had not had any vaccinations under the [Universal Immunization Programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_Immunization_Programme) as of 2005.[^cite_note-healthsurvey-136]<sup>: 48 </sup> Kolkata ranked second with access to an *[anganwadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anganwadi)* centre under the [Integrated Child Development Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integrated_Child_Development_Services) (ICDS) programme for 57% of the children between 0 and 71 months.[^cite_note-healthsurvey-136]<sup>: 51 </sup> The proportion of [malnourished](258979.md), [anaemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anaemia) and [underweight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Underweight) children in Kolkata was less in comparison to other surveyed cities.[^cite_note-healthsurvey-136]<sup>: 54–55 </sup>

About 18% of the men and 30% of the women in Kolkata are [obese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obese)—the majority of them belonging to the non-poor strata of society.[^cite_note-healthsurvey-136]<sup>: 105 </sup> In 2005, Kolkata had the highest percentage (55%) among the surveyed cities of [anaemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anemia) women, while 20% of the men in Kolkata were anaemic.[^cite_note-healthsurvey-136]<sup>: 56–57 </sup> Diseases like [diabetes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diabetes), [asthma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asthma), [goitre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goitre) and other [thyroid disorders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thyroid#Clinical_significance) were found in large numbers of people.[^cite_note-healthsurvey-136]<sup>: 57–59 </sup> Tropical diseases like [malaria](20423.md), [dengue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dengue) and *[chikungunya](366112.md)* are prevalent in Kolkata, though their incidence is decreasing.[^cite_note-234][^cite_note-235] Kolkata is one of the districts in India with [a high number of people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIV/AIDS_in_India) with [AIDS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIV/AIDS); it has been designated a district prone to high risk.[^cite_note-236][^cite_note-237] As of 2014, because of [higher air pollution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_pollution_in_India), the [life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancy) of a person born in the city is four years fewer than in the suburbs.[^cite_note-238]

## Education



[University of Calcutta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Calcutta)

Kolkata's schools are run by the state government or private organisations, many of which are religious. [Bengali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_language) and English are the primary languages of instruction; [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu) and [Hindi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindi) are also used, particularly in central Kolkata.[^cite_note-educationsystem-239][^cite_note-240] Schools in Kolkata follow the ["10+2+3" plan](293121.md). After completing their secondary education, students typically enroll in schools that have a higher secondary facility and are affiliated with the [West Bengal Council of Higher Secondary Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Bengal_Council_of_Higher_Secondary_Education), the [ICSE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Certificate_of_Secondary_Education), or the [CBSE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Board_of_Secondary_Education).[^cite_note-educationsystem-239] They usually choose a focus on liberal arts, business, or science. Vocational programs are also available.[^cite_note-educationsystem-239] Some Kolkata schools, for example [South Point School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Point_School), [La Martinière Calcutta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Martini%C3%A8re_Calcutta), [Calcutta Boys' School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calcutta_Boys'_School), [St. James' School (Kolkata)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._James'_School_\(Kolkata\)), [St. Xavier's Collegiate School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Xavier's_Collegiate_School) and [Loreto House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loreto_House), have been ranked amongst the best schools in the country.[^cite_note-241]

As of 2010, the Kolkata urban agglomeration is home to 14 universities run by the state government.[^cite_note-higheredu124127-242] The colleges are each affiliated with a university or institution based either in Kolkata or elsewhere in India. [Aliah University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aliah_University) which was founded in 1780 as *Mohammedan College of Calcutta* is the oldest post-secondary educational institution of the city.[^cite_note-Home_page-243] The [University of Calcutta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Calcutta), founded in 1857, is the first modern university in South Asia.[^cite_note-higheredu129-244] [Presidency College, Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_College,_Kolkata) (formerly Hindu College between 1817 and 1855), founded in 1855, was one of the oldest colleges in India. It was affiliated with the University of Calcutta until 2010 when it was converted to [Presidency University, Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_University,_Kolkata) in 2010. [Bengal Engineering and Science University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengal_Engineering_and_Science_University,_Shibpur) (BESU) is the second oldest engineering institution of the country located in Howrah.[^cite_note-245] An [Institute of National Importance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_National_Importance), BESU was converted to India's first [IIEST](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Institutes_of_Engineering_Science_and_Technology). [Jadavpur University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jadavpur_University) is known for its arts, science, and engineering faculties.[^cite_note-246] The [Indian Institute of Management Calcutta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Institute_of_Management_Calcutta), which was the first of the [Indian Institutes of Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Institutes_of_Management), was established in 1961 at [Joka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joka,_Kolkata), a locality in the south-western suburbs. Kolkata also houses the [Indian Institute of Foreign Trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Institute_of_Foreign_Trade), which was started here in 2006.[^cite_note-247]



[Presidency University, Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_University,_Kolkata)

The [West Bengal National University of Juridical Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Bengal_National_University_of_Juridical_Sciences) is one of India's [autonomous law schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomous_law_schools_in_India),[^cite_note-248][^cite_note-249] and the [Indian Statistical Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Statistical_Institute) is a public research institute and university. State owned [Maulana Abul Kalam Azad University of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maulana_Abul_Kalam_Azad_University_of_Technology), West Bengal (MAKAUT, WB), formerly West Bengal University of Technology (WBUT) is the largest Technological University in terms of student enrollment and number of Institutions affiliated by it. Private institutions include the [Ramakrishna Mission Vivekananda Educational and Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramakrishna_Mission_Vivekananda_Educational_and_Research_Institute) and [University of Engineering & Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Engineering_&_Management,_Kolkata) (UEM).

### Notable scholars

Notable scholars who were born, worked or studied in Kolkata include physicists [Satyendra Nath Bose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satyendra_Nath_Bose), [Meghnad Saha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meghnad_Saha),[^cite_note-calunivalumni-250] and [Jagadish Chandra Bose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagadish_Chandra_Bose);[^cite_note-calunivteachers-251] chemist [Prafulla Chandra Ray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prafulla_Chandra_Ray);[^cite_note-calunivalumni-250] statisticians [Prasanta Chandra Mahalanobis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prasanta_Chandra_Mahalanobis) and [Anil Kumar Gain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anil_Kumar_Gain);[^cite_note-calunivalumni-250] physician [Upendranath Brahmachari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upendranath_Brahmachari);[^cite_note-calunivalumni-250] educator [Ashutosh Mukherjee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashutosh_Mukherjee);[^cite_note-252] and Nobel laureates [Rabindranath Tagore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabindranath_Tagore),[^cite_note-253] [C. V. Raman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C._V._Raman),[^cite_note-calunivteachers-251] and [Amartya Sen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amartya_Sen).[^cite_note-254]

### Research institutes

Kolkata houses many research institutes, including the following:

- [All India Institute of Hygiene and Public Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_India_Institute_of_Hygiene_and_Public_Health)

- [Bose Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bose_Institute)

- [Central Glass and Ceramic Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Glass_and_Ceramic_Research_Institute) (CGCRI)

- [Centre for Studies in Social Sciences, Calcutta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre_for_Studies_in_Social_Sciences,_Calcutta)

- Dr. B C Roy Post Graduate Institute of Paediatric Sciences

- [Indian Association for the Cultivation of Science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Association_for_the_Cultivation_of_Science) (IACS)&#x20;

  - Nobel laureate [Sir C. V. Raman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sir_C._V._Raman) did his groundbreaking work in [Raman effect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raman_effect) at IACS.

- [Indian Centre for Space Physics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Centre_for_Space_Physics)

- [Indian Institute of Chemical Biology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Institute_of_Chemical_Biology) (IICB)

- [Indian Institute of Science Education and Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Institute_of_Science_Education_and_Research) (IISER)

- [Indian Institute of Social Welfare and Business Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Institute_of_Social_Welfare_and_Business_Management) (IISWBM)

- [National Institute of Pharmaceutical Education and Research, Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_of_Pharmaceutical_Education_and_Research,_Kolkata)

- [Saha Institute of Nuclear Physics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saha_Institute_of_Nuclear_Physics) (SINP)

- [S. N. Bose National Centre for Basic Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S._N._Bose_National_Centre_for_Basic_Sciences) (SNBNCBS)

- [Variable Energy Cyclotron Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Variable_Energy_Cyclotron_Centre) (VECC)

## Culture

Main articles: [Culture of Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Kolkata) and [Durga Puja in Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durga_Puja_in_Kolkata)

See also: [Street food of Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Street_food_of_Kolkata)



[Durga Puja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durga_Puja) is the biggest festival for [Bengali Hindus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_Hindus).



Durga Puja pandals in Kolkata often have grand designs.



[Vijayadashami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijayadashami) in [Tollygunge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tollygunge)



Victoria Memorial at night

Kolkata is known for its literary, artistic and revolutionary heritage; as the former capital of India, it was the birthplace of modern Indian literary and artistic thought.[^cite_note-niradchaudh-255] Kolkata has been called the "City of Furious, Creative Energy"[^cite_note-sinha-256] as well as the "cultural \[or literary] capital of India".[^cite_note-257][^cite_note-nobleliterary-258] The presence of *[paras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Para_\(Bengali\))*, which are neighbourhoods that possess a strong sense of community, is characteristic of the city.[^cite_note-parawbgov-259] Typically, each *para* has its own community club and on occasion, a playing field.[^cite_note-parawbgov-259] Residents engage in *[addas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adda_\(Indian\))*, or leisurely chats, that often take the form of freestyle intellectual conversation.[^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261] The city has a tradition of political [graffiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graffiti) depicting everything from outrageous slander to witty banter and limericks, caricatures and propaganda.[^cite_note-262][^cite_note-263]

Kolkata has many buildings adorned with [Indo-Islamic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Islamic_architecture) and [Indo-Saracenic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Saracenic_architecture) architectural motifs. Several well-maintained major buildings from the colonial period have been declared "heritage structures";[^cite_note-264] others are in various stages of decay.[^cite_note-265][^cite_note-266] Established in 1814 as the nation's oldest museum, the [Indian Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Museum) houses large collections that showcase [Indian natural history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_natural_history) and [Indian art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_art).[^cite_note-267] [Marble Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marble_Palace_\(Kolkata\)) is a classic example of a European mansion that was built in the city. The [Victoria Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Memorial,_Kolkata), a [place of interest in Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Places_of_interest_in_Kolkata), has a museum documenting the city's history. The [National Library of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Library_of_India) is the leading public library in the country while [Science City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_City,_Kolkata) is the largest science centre in the [Indian subcontinent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_subcontinent).[^cite_note-268]

Large white rectangular building with tall arched windows

[National Library of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Library_of_India)&#x20;

The popularity of commercial theatres in the city has declined since the 1980s.[^cite_note-bhattawomen-269]<sup>: 99 </sup>[^cite_note-dett5312009-270] [Group theatres of Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_theatres_of_Kolkata), a cultural movement that started in the 1940s contrasting with the then-popular commercial theatres, are theatres that are not professional or commercial, and are centres of various experiments in theme, content, and production;[^cite_note-heierstad39-271] group theatres use the [proscenium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proscenium) stage to highlight socially relevant messages.[^cite_note-bhattawomen-269]<sup>: 99 </sup>[^cite_note-272] [Chitpur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chitpur) locality of the city houses multiple production companies of *[jatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jatra_\(Bengal\))*, a tradition of folk drama popular in rural Bengal.[^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274] Kolkata is the home of the [Bengali cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_West_Bengal) industry, dubbed "Tollywood" for [Tollygunj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tollygunj), where most of the state's film studios are located.[^cite_note-Sarkar-275] [Its long tradition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parallel_cinema) of [art films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_film) includes globally acclaimed film directors such as [Satyajit Ray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satyajit_Ray), [Ritwik Ghatak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ritwik_Ghatak), [Mrinal Sen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mrinal_Sen), [Tapan Sinha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tapan_Sinha) and contemporary directors such as [Aparna Sen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aparna_Sen), [Buddhadeb Dasgupta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhadeb_Dasgupta), [Goutam Ghose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goutam_Ghose) and [Rituparno Ghosh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rituparno_Ghosh).[^cite_note-276]

During the 19th and 20th centuries, [Bengali literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_literature) was modernised through the works of authors such as [Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ishwar_Chandra_Vidyasagar), [Bankim Chandra Chatterjee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bankim_Chandra_Chatterjee), [Michael Madhusudan Dutt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Madhusudan_Dutt), [Rabindranath Tagore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabindranath_Tagore), [Kazi Nazrul Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazi_Nazrul_Islam) and [Sarat Chandra Chattopadhyay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarat_Chandra_Chattopadhyay).[^cite_note-277] Coupled with social reforms led by [Raja Ram Mohan Roy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raja_Ram_Mohan_Roy), [Swami Vivekananda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swami_Vivekananda) and others, this constituted a major part of the [Bengal Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengal_Renaissance).[^cite_note-278] The middle and latter parts of the 20th century witnessed the arrival of post-modernism, as well as literary movements such as those espoused by the *[Kallol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kallol)* movement, [hungryalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungry_generation) and the [little magazines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_magazine_movement#Bengali_little_magazine_movement).[^cite_note-279] Large majority of publishers of the city is concentrated in and around [College Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_Street_\(Kolkata\)), "a half-mile of bookshops and bookstalls spilling over onto the pavement", selling new and used books.[^cite_note-smithsonian_July_91-280]



Sandalwood [Durga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durga) in the [Indian Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Museum)

[Kalighat painting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalighat_painting) originated in 19th-century Kolkata as a local style that reflected a variety of themes including mythology and quotidian life.[^cite_note-kalighpaint1-281] The [Government College of Art and Craft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_College_of_Art_&_Craft), founded in 1864, has been the cradle as well as workplace of eminent artists including [Abanindranath Tagore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abanindranath_Tagore), [Jamini Roy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamini_Roy) and [Nandalal Bose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nandalal_Bose).[^cite_note-282] The art college was the birthplace of the [Bengal school of art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengal_school_of_art) that arose as an [avant garde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avant_garde) and nationalist movement reacting against the prevalent [academic art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academic_art) styles in the early 20th century.[^cite_note-benartmitter-283][^cite_note-benartatlas-284] The [Academy of Fine Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_of_Fine_Arts,_Kolkata) and other art galleries hold regular art exhibitions. The city is recognised for its appreciation of *[Rabindra Sangeet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabindra_Sangeet)* (songs written by Rabindranath Tagore) and [Indian classical music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_classical_music), with important concerts and recitals, such as [Dover Lane Music Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dover_Lane_Music_Conference), being held throughout the year; Bengali popular music, including [*baul* folk ballads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baul), *[kirtans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirtan)* and *[Gajan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gajan_\(festival\))* festival music; and modern music, including Bengali-language *adhunik* songs.[^cite_note-banglasong-285][^cite_note-286] Since the early 1990s, [new genres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_rock#Rock_scenes) have emerged, including one comprising alternative folk–rock [Bengali bands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_music_of_West_Bengal).[^cite_note-banglasong-285] Another new style, *jibonmukhi gaan* ("songs about life"), is based on [realism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Realism_\(arts\)).[^cite_note-bhattawomen-269]<sup>: 105 </sup>

Sandesh varieties

[*Sandesh*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandesh_\(confectionery\)), a typical Bengali sweet made from [chhena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chhena)

Key elements of [Kolkata's cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_cuisine) include rice and a fish curry known as *machher jhol*,[^cite_note-machhe-287] which can be accompanied by desserts such as *[roshogolla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rasgulla)*, *[sandesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandesh_\(confectionery\))*, and a sweet yoghurt known as *[mishti dohi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitha_Dahi)*. Bengal's large repertoire of seafood dishes includes various preparations of *[ilish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilish)*, a fish that is a favourite among Calcuttans. Street foods such as *[beguni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beguni)* (fried battered eggplant slices), [*kati* roll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kati_roll) (flatbread roll with vegetable or chicken, mutton or egg stuffing), *[phuchka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panipuri)* (a deep-fried crêpe with tamarind sauce) and [Indian Chinese cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Chinese_cuisine) from Chinatown are popular.[^cite_note-kolkataimages-288][^cite_note-rolltelegraph-289][^cite_note-290][^cite_note-291]

Four women wearing saree in different dancing poses

Dance accompanied by [Rabindra Sangeet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabindra_Sangeet), a music genre started by Rabindranath Tagore

Though Bengali women traditionally wear the *[sari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sari)*, the *[shalwar kameez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shalwar_kameez)* and Western attire is gaining acceptance among younger women.[^cite_note-292] Western-style dress has greater acceptance among men, although the traditional *[dhoti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhoti)* and *[kurta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurta)* are seen during festivals. [Durga Puja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durga_Puja), held in September–October, is Kolkata's most important and largest festival; it is an occasion for glamorous celebrations and artistic decorations.[^cite_note-293][^cite_note-294] The Bengali New Year, known as [Poila Boishak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pohela_Boishakh), as well as the harvest festival of Poush Parbon are among the city's other festivals; also celebrated are [Kali Puja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kali_Puja), [Diwali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diwali), [Chhaith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chhath),[^cite_note-295] [Jitiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jitiya), [Holi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holi), [Jagaddhatri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagaddhatri) Puja, [Saraswati Puja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saraswati_Puja), [Rathayatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rathayatra), [Janmashtami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janmashtami), [Maha Shivratri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maha_Shivratri), [Vishwakarma Puja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vishwakarma_Puja), [Lakshmi Puja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lakshmi_Puja), [Ganesh Chathurthi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ganesh_Chathurthi), [Makar Sankranti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makar_Sankranti), [Gajan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gajan_\(festival\)), [Kalpataru Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalpataru_Day), [Bhai Phonta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhai_Phonta), Maghotsab, [Eid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eid_ul-Fitr), [Muharram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muharram), [Christmas](6237.md), [Buddha Purnima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddha_Purnima) and [Mahavir Jayanti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahavir_Jayanti). Cultural events include the [Rabindra Jayanti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabindra_Jayanti), [Independence Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Day_\(India\)) (15 August), [Republic Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_Day) (26 January), [Kolkata Book Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_Book_Fair), the Dover Lane Music Festival, the [Kolkata International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_International_Film_Festival), [Nandikar's National Theatre Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nandikar's_National_Theatre_Festival), [Statesman Vintage & Classic Car Rally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statesman_Vintage_&_Classic_Car_Rally) and [Gandhi Jayanti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gandhi_Jayanti).

## Media

See also: [Kolkata in the media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_in_the_media) and [List of Bengali-language television channels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Bengali-language_television_channels)

A five-storied building in cream colour with multiple columns in front

Akashvani Bhawan, the head office of state-owned [All India Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_India_Radio), Kolkata

The first newspaper in India, the *[Bengal Gazette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hicky's_Bengal_Gazette)* started publishing from the city in 1780.[^cite_note-Eaman2009-296] Among Kolkata's widely circulated Bengali-language newspapers are *[Anandabazar Patrika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anandabazar_Patrika)*, *[Bartaman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bartaman)*, [Ei Samay Sangbadpatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ei_Samay_Sangbadpatra), *[Sangbad Pratidin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangbad_Pratidin)*, *[Aajkaal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aajkaal)*, *[Dainik Statesman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dainik_Statesman)* and *[Ganashakti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ganashakti)*.[^cite_note-newspaper-297] *[The Statesman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Statesman_\(India\))* and *[The Telegraph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Telegraph_\(India\))* are two major English-language newspapers that are produced and published from the city. Other popular English-language newspapers published and sold in the city include *[The Times of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times_of_India)*, *[Hindustan Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindustan_Times)*, *[The Hindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hindu)*, *[The Indian Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Indian_Express)* and *[The Asian Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Asian_Age)*.[^cite_note-newspaper-297] As the largest trading centre in East India, the city has several high-circulation financial dailies, including *[The Economic Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economic_Times)*, *[The Financial Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Financial_Express_\(India\))*, *[Business Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_Line)* and *[Business Standard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_Standard)*.[^cite_note-newspaper-297][^cite_note-298] Vernacular newspapers, such as those in the [Hindi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindi), [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu), [Gujarati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gujarati_language), [Odia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odia_language), [Punjabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punjabi_language) and Chinese languages, are read by minorities.[^cite_note-BanerjeePage10-145][^cite_note-newspaper-297] Major periodicals based in the city include *[Desh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desh_\(magazine\))*, *[Sananda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sananda_\(magazine\))*, *[Saptahik Bartaman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saptahik_Bartaman)*, *[Unish-Kuri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unish-Kuri)*, *[Anandalok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anandalok)* and *[Anandamela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anandamela)*.[^cite_note-newspaper-297] Historically, Kolkata has been the centre of the [Bengali little magazine movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_magazine_movement#Bengali_little_magazine_movement).[^cite_note-299][^cite_note-300]

[All India Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_India_Radio) (AIR), the national state-owned radio broadcaster, airs several [AM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AM_broadcasting) radio stations in the city. Kolkata has [10 local radio stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_FM_radio_stations_in_India#Kolkata) broadcasting on [FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frequency_modulation), including three from AIR. India's state-owned television broadcaster, [Doordarshan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doordarshan), provides two free-to-air terrestrial channels,[^cite_note-301] while a mix of Bengali, Hindi, English, and other regional channels are accessible via [cable subscription](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cable_television), [direct-broadcast satellite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct-broadcast_satellite) services, or [internet-based television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPTV).[^cite_note-302][^cite_note-303][^cite_note-304] [Bengali-language 24-hour television news channels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Bengali-language_television_channels#News_channels) include [ABP Ananda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABP_Ananda), [News18 Bangla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/News18_Bangla), [Kolkata TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_TV), [Zee 24 Ghanta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zee_24_Ghanta), TV9 Bangla and [Republic Bangla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_Bangla).[^cite_note-305]

## Sports

See also: [Football in Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_in_Kolkata), [Kolkata Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_Marathon), and [Kolkata Derby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_Derby)



[Salt Lake Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vivekananda_Yuba_Bharati_Krirangan) ([Greater Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_Metropolitan_Area)) on a matchday of the [2017 FIFA U-17 World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_FIFA_U-17_World_Cup)

The most popular sports in Kolkata are [football](10568.md) and [cricket](25675557.md). Unlike most parts of India, the residents show significant enthusiasm for football.[^cite_note-306] [Indian Football Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Football_Association), the oldest football association of the country is based here. It administers football in West Bengal. Kolkata is home to India's top football clubs such as [Mohun Bagan AC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohun_Bagan_AC), [East Bengal Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Bengal_Club) and the [Mohammedan SC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammedan_SC_\(Kolkata\)).[^cite_note-307][^cite_note-308] The [Calcutta Football League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calcutta_Football_League), the oldest football league in Asia, was started in 1898. Mohun Bagan AC, one of the oldest football clubs in Asia, is the only organisation to be dubbed as "National Club of India".[^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310] Two clubs of the city - [Mohun Bagan Super Giant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohun_Bagan_Super_Giant) and [East Bengal FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Bengal_FC) compete in the [Indian Super League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Super_League) (ISL). Football matches between Mohun Bagan and East Bengal, called as the [Kolkata Derby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_Derby), witness large audience attendance and rivalry between patrons.[^cite_note-311] The multi-use [Salt Lake Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salt_Lake_Stadium), also known as Vivekananda Yuba Bharati Krirangan, is India's second largest stadium by [seating capacity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seating_capacity). Most matches of the [2017 FIFA U-17 World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_FIFA_U-17_World_Cup) were played in this stadium including both Semi-final matches and the Final match. Kolkata also accounted for 45% of total attendance in 2017 FIFA U-17 World Cup with an average of 55,345 spectators.[^cite_note-312] The [Calcutta Cricket and Football Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calcutta_Cricket_and_Football_Club) is the second-oldest cricket club in the world.[^cite_note-313][^cite_note-ccfcdate-314]



Established in 1864, Eden Gardens is the oldest cricket stadium in India.

As in the rest of India, cricket is popular in Kolkata and is played on various grounds throughout the city.[^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316] Kolkata is home to [Indian Premier League](13260340.md) (IPL) franchise [Kolkata Knight Riders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolkata_Knight_Riders) and also the [Cricket Association of Bengal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cricket_Association_of_Bengal) which regulates cricket in West Bengal and the [Bengal cricket team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengal_cricket_team). Tournaments, especially those involving cricket, football, badminton and [carrom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrom), are regularly organised here on an inter-locality or inter-club basis.[^cite_note-parawbgov-259] The Maidan, a vast field that serves as the city's largest park, hosts several minor football and cricket clubs and coaching institutes.[^cite_note-317] [Eden Gardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eden_Gardens), which has a capacity of 80,000, as of 2017,[^cite_note-edenstats-318] hosted the final match of the [1987 Cricket World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1987_Cricket_World_Cup).

The [Netaji Indoor Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netaji_Indoor_Stadium) served as host of the [1981 Asian Basketball Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1981_Asian_Basketball_Championship), where [India's national basketball team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India's_national_basketball_team) finished 5th, ahead of teams that belong to Asia's basketball elite, such as [Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_national_basketball_team). The city has three 18-hole golf courses. The oldest is at the [Royal Calcutta Golf Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Calcutta_Golf_Club), the first golf club built outside the United Kingdom.[^cite_note-319][^cite_note-320] The other two are located at the [Tollygunge Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tollygunge_Club) and at [Fort William](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_William,_India). The [Royal Calcutta Turf Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Calcutta_Turf_Club) hosts horse racing and polo matches.[^cite_note-321] The [Calcutta Polo Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calcutta_Polo_Club) is considered the oldest extant polo club in the world.[^cite_note-322][^cite_note-323][^cite_note-Kolpolo-324] The [Calcutta Racket Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calcutta_Racket_Club) is a [squash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Squash_\(sport\)) and [racquet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racquet) club in Kolkata. It was founded in 1793, making it one of the oldest rackets clubs in the world, and the first in the Indian subcontinent.[^cite_note-325][^cite_note-326] The [Calcutta South Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calcutta_South_Club) is a venue for national and international tennis tournaments; it held the first grass-court national championship in 1946.[^cite_note-327][^cite_note-328] In the period 2005–2007, [Sunfeast Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunfeast_Open), a tier-III tournament on the [Women's Tennis Association](57768.md) circuit, was held in the Netaji Indoor Stadium; it has since been discontinued.[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330]

The [Calcutta Rowing Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calcutta_Rowing_Club) hosts [rowing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_rowing) heats and training events. Kolkata, considered the leading centre of [rugby union in India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rugby_union_in_India), gives its name to the oldest international tournament in rugby union, the [Calcutta Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calcutta_Cup).[^cite_note-331][^cite_note-332][^cite_note-333] The Automobile Association of Eastern India, established in 1904,[^cite_note-334][^cite_note-335] and the Bengal Motor Sports Club are involved in promoting motor sports and car rallies in Kolkata and West Bengal.[^cite_note-bmscaaei-336][^cite_note-337] The [Beighton Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beighton_Cup), an event organised by the Bengal Hockey Association and first played in 1895, is India's oldest [field hockey](10886.md) tournament; it is usually held on the [Mohun Bagan Ground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohun_Bagan_Ground) of the Maidan.[^cite_note-beightontelegraph-338][^cite_note-339] Athletes from Kolkata include [Sourav Ganguly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sourav_Ganguly), [Pankaj Roy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pankaj_Roy) and [Jhulan Goswami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jhulan_Goswami), who are former [captains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_India_national_cricket_captains) of the [Indian national cricket team](407754.md); [Olympic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_at_the_Olympics) tennis bronze medalist [Leander Paes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leander_Paes), golfer [Arjun Atwal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arjun_Atwal), and former footballers [Sailen Manna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sailen_Manna), [Chuni Goswami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuni_Goswami), [P. K. Banerjee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P._K._Banerjee) and [Subrata Bhattacharya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subrata_Bhattacharya).

## Notable people

## International relations

### Foreign missions

There are 70 diplomatic missions in Kolkata, of which 24 are consulate missions, two are high commissions and the rest are honorary consulates.[^cite_note-340] The [U.S. Consulate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Consulate) in Kolkata dates from 19 November 1792 and is the [U.S. Department of State](31975.md)'s second oldest consulate in the world and the oldest U.S. consulate in [India](14533.md).[^cite_note-341] The [Foreigners Regional Registration Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreigners_Regional_Registration_Office) (FRRO) is in charge of immigration and registration activities in the city.[^cite_note-342]
